(part 1 (scope) LANDED 2026-09-22 / part 2 (current-agent+modelID + dead-mark) LANDED 2026-09-23 / part 3 (Unit-2 ctx-line-suffix + Direct gates) LANDED 2026-09-23; live verification PENDING post-restart, HIGH priority — maintainer call) — auto_resume unbounded session-spawn loop\n294: - **Problem + evidence:** on 2026-09-22 (~19:57–20:00Z) the LIVE auto_resume\n295:   plugin created a NEW session every ~10s (every 2nd recovery attempt — 2\n296:   attempts per session at 5s each, confirmed by the session naming) —\n297:   auto_resume.log shows the `ses_f354e4cb… / ses_f354e259… / ses_f354dfe7… / …`\n298:   series, each logged as `scope= planner` + `recovery= attempt 1` then\n299:   `attempt 2`, each failing with `UnknownError` at `SessionPrompt.createUserMessage`.\n300:   The maintainer saw `UnknownError` every 5s in the terminal and had to RESTART\n301:   opencode to stop it. (Diagnosed from auto_resume.log this turn, 2026-09-22.)\n302: - **Desired outcome:** auto_resume must never create sessions unboundedly. A\n303:   spawn / continue / recovery that keeps failing (or keeps re-triggering on\n304:   freshly-spawned sessions) must STOP: a GLOBAL cap (not just per-session) + a\n305:   dead-mark (a session whose recovery/continue attempt fails is marked dead and\n306:   skipped on later cycles), and freshly auto-spawned sessions must NOT be\n307:   re-scoped as in-scope planners.\n308: - **Acceptance criteria:** (a) under load a failing spawn/continue is globally\n309:   capped and the offending session is dead-marked + skipped (no per-cycle\n310:   retry); (b) auto-spawned sessions are not re-scoped as planners; (c) an\n311:   `UnknownError` on a failed `createUserMessage` discards the session (no\n312:   retry loop); (d) a test/repro showing the loop stops after the cap.\n313: - **Suggested scope:** `.opencode/plugin/auto_resume.ts` — the spawn path\n314:   (unit-4 \"restart→spawn\") + the recovery path; the global cap + dead-mark; the\n315:   scope logic. TIES IN with the earlier design issue (unit-4 scope / the\n316:   spurious worker resume / the action-line dilemma) — all resolved by one scope\n317:   refinement (below).\n318: - **GENERALIZED SCOPE (his ruling 2026-09-22):** unit-4 scope = actual PLANNER\n319:   sessions (always) ∪ sessions started/toggled via `<|Autorun|>` (the #82\n320:   own-line toggle). NO new marker needed — the #82 toggle already gates scope\n321:   ON/OFF; unit 4 just follows it for ANY agent type (drop the planner-only\n322:   gate). This lets him run other agents (prompt_builder, a future researcher,\n323:   etc.) in a loop by toggling them with `<|Autorun|>`. Bonus: freshly-spawned\n324:   sessions + unmarked worker sessions stay OUT of scope (not a planner, not\n325:   `<|Autorun|>`-marked) → fixes the #85 loop AND the spurious worker-resume.\n326:  - **Status:** part 1 (scope) LANDED 2026-09-22 (worker worker_Q3S_170K —\n327:    the #82 generalized scope in auto_resume.ts + smoke: real-planner-via-\n328:    agent-field ∪ last-own-line-toggle-ON for ANY agent type; the `spawned`\n329:    self-mark is now an EXCLUSION, so a self-spawned successor is never\n330:     re-scoped → the #85 loop condition is gone — smoke 97/97 + gate green:\n331:     probe 241/241, all smokes, pytest 459+1w, ruff F=0; the commit hash is\n332:     recorded by the planner in the follow-up bookkeeping commit). PART 2\n333:     (current-agent+modelID in the injected bodies + dead-mark on a failed\n334:     send) LANDED 2026-09-23 (worker worker_Q3S_170K — the hardcoded\n335:     PLANNER_AGENT_ID is replaced by the session's CURRENT agent+modelID,\n336:     resolved at fire-time: last-assistant info → opencode.jsonc agent-\n337:     config fallback (cached) → host default (NEVER a planner constant); a\n338:     failed CONTINUE dead-marks the idle cycle — remaining retries + the\n339:      cap-exhaustion fallback spawn are skipped (no doomed successor),\n340:      cleared on a fresh busy — auto_resume smoke 105/105; the commit hash\n341:      is recorded by the planner in the follow-up bookkeeping commit).\n342:      PART 3 (the live test exposed the Unit-2 tick design: re-fire on\n343:      stale armed sessions + a self-loop) LANDED 2026-09-23 (worker\n344:      worker_Q3S_170K — the Unit-2 tick leg is REMOVED: the nudge is a\n345:      PASSIVE ctx-line SUFFIX on the session's OWN tool-call return (the\n346:      gauge plugin's ctx: line channel — tool.execute.after), per busy\n347:      session, ladder (>= 0.95 \"self-compact now\" / >= 0.98 --maintainer-\n348:      flagged), scope \"none\" (Direct) suppresses it (c); NO promptAsync on\n349:      the Unit-2 path (no resume, no loop, stale sessions unreachable by\n350:      construction); scopeVerdict checks the LAST OWN-LINE TOGGLE FIRST —\n351:      Direct deactivates Unit 4 for the planner (d); the Unit-4 scope is\n352:       unchanged — auto_resume smoke 102/102 (baseline 105: the old\n353:       promptAsync-era checks adapted to the passive mechanism) — ded7245\n354:       (code + smoke + TODO) + 5992f38 (handover), recorded by planner-8 in\n355:       the 2026-09-23 bookkeeping commit). (open, 2026-09-23, planner-12 direct; live incident 14:44–14:46Z; HIGH) compaction summary leaks into spawn identity + unit-4 routing (agent=compaction successor; mis-route on a quoted action line)\n395: - **Problem / evidence:** live incident 2026-09-23 14:44–14:46Z (pre-#90 build d2b9d510): planner-11 (ses_f318f0d77…) self-compacted (14:44:19Z) → the compaction summary is stored as an ASSISTANT message with info.agent=compaction + info.model=<session model> (the same-model fallback — agent.compaction.model is commented out). The summary text QUOTES \"Previous planner-10 … closed with `action: restart`\" (6612 chars). The unit-4 tick (14:46:06Z) then: (1) `lastAssistantAction` scanned the summary (the last assistant message) → found the QUOTED `action: restart` → ROUTE= RESTART SPAWN (mis-route — the planner's actual last real turn, 14:44:12Z, was a 76-char no-action-line close; the loop log records \"self-compact + unit-4 resume per maintainer inbox\"); (2) `resolveInjectIdentity` → the summary's info → the spawn body agent=compaction + model=Qwen3.8-27B-Q3S-170K (the spawn= line 14:46:06.647Z). \"compaction\" is NOT an agent defined in the live opencode.jsonc → the successor (ses_f3144d9d6…, ident=…planner-12) ran with the WRONG agent + mode (maintainer: \"i started the session prior with autorun but the wrong agent and mode. thus reverted and then started you with direct\"). DB evidence: the ses_f318f0d77 message 14:44:19.866Z (assistant / agent=compaction) + the auto_resume.log lines 14:46:06.629–647Z.\n396: - **STILL LATENT in the live build (ef8c6149 = #90):** `lastAssistantInfo` (L921-930) + `resolveInjectIdentity` (L1052-1060) + `lastAssistantAction` (L1065-1077) do NOT skip the compaction agent → recurs whenever an autorun session self-compacts immediately before the idle routing decision (the near-limit protocol makes this common): the successor is spawned with the wrong agent, AND the routing reads action lines QUOTED in the summary instead of the real close.\n397: - **Desired outcome:** identity + routing resolve from the last REAL turn (the compaction summary is never used for either).\n398: - **Fix design (recommended — maintainer call, observable behavior change):** skip assistant messages with info.agent === \"compaction\" in BOTH `lastAssistantInfo` (→ the spawn/inject identity falls back to the last real assistant message: the planner's agent+model) and `lastAssistantAction` (→ the routing reads the pre-compact close: a no-action-line Work State dump → recovery, as intended). One guard each; smoke fixture: a messages() shape whose last assistant message = a compaction summary (agent=compaction, text quoting \"action: restart\") → (a) the routing returns null (recovery), (b) the spawn body agent = the preceding real assistant's agent.\n399: - **Acceptance criteria:** the smoke pins above + gate green; LIVE: the next autorun self-compact→idle cycle (a) routes correctly (no mis-spawn from a quoted line), (b) the spawn= line carries agent=planner_* (not compaction).\n400: - **Suggested scope:** `.opencode/plugin/auto_resume.ts` (L921-930, L1052-1060, L1065-1077), `.opencode/plugin/tests/auto_resume.smoke.mjs`.\n401: - **Status:** fix LANDED 2026-09-23 (planner-12 direct, planner-implemented after his \"approved\") — the one-guard-each fix per the design (skip agent=\"compaction\" in `lastAssistantInfo` + `lastAssistantAction`, auto_resume.ts); smoke re-pinned 121/121 (+3 #91 pins e1/e2) + gate green (probe 241/241, all smokes, pytest 459+1w, ruff F=0). Commit **2fa4bb6**. REMAINING — live acceptance (needs his restart to the new build + the next autorun cycle): a self-compact→idle cycle must (a) route from the real close (no mis-spawn from a quoted line), (b) the spawn= line carries agent=planner_* (not compaction) — this also completes the #90 new-build spawn tail (DONE 2026-09-23). **Spawn-tail live acceptance (planner-13, build v=7d2e6207 live, process start 19:13:25Z):** the `spawn=` line carried `agent=planner_Q3S_170K` (the real agent, not compaction) and `route= restart spawn` came from the real close — no mis-route off the quoted 17:32Z compaction summary in this episode. NOT YET live-discriminated: the failure case needs the compaction summary to be the LAST evaluated turn (a self-compact then idle cycle in an autorun-scoped session) — the maintainer's re-engagement + own-line toggle + real close all followed the 17:32Z summary, so the guard was not the deciding factor here; the guard is smoke-covered (3 pins) and the discriminating cycle will occur naturally under the near-limit protocol.\n402: \n403: ## #92. (LANDED 2026-09-27, 2026-09-23, planner-12 direct; his question; SMALL) pre-compaction hook should save BOTH the lossless full markdown AND a raw `--json` snapshot\n404: - **Problem / evidence:** after #78 the hook keeps only the markdown backup. His question (2026-09-23): with raw JSON as the on-demand `--json` mode (DB-sourced), how do you get the pre-compaction JSON later — \"the db is overwritten by the compacted session\"? MEASURED on this session (compacted 17:32:02Z): the DB is NOT overwritten — 36 pre-compaction messages + 170 parts are still in the DB, 76 reasoning parts whole-session (the compaction summary is an ADDED assistant message, agent=compaction; planner-11's session showed the same). So on-demand `--json` of a compacted session works TODAY. BUT the DB is a live host-managed store (session deletion / pruning / migration / corruption can lose the rows) — the hook's _c0 snapshot is the only PINNED \"state at compaction time\" record; markdown-only forfeits the lossless source at exactly that moment.\n405: - **Desired outcome:** every pre-compaction dump writes BOTH: the lossless full markdown (current) + a raw `--json` snapshot (the lossless master — any later filtered view re-derivable from it).\n406: - **Acceptance criteria:** two DUMP-OK lines per dump (md + json); both files land in the archive dir; the #78 diagnostics (ms= / DUMP-RETRY= / DUMP-FAIL + stderr) + the 120 s budget + one retry apply to BOTH dumps; compact_memory smoke re-pinned; gate green.\n407: - **Suggested scope:** `.opencode/plugin/compact_memory.ts` (the preCompactionDump call site — the dump script already has `--json`), `.opencode/plugin/tests/compact_memory.smoke.mjs`.\n408: - **Status:** LANDED 2026-09-27 (worker-26, plan26 — per the 2026-09-27 ruling \"save both\"): `preCompactionDump` now writes BOTH artifacts — the lossless md (first) + the raw `--json` snapshot (the lossless master) — each INDEPENDENTLY (the same attempt → DUMP-RETRY=1 → one retry → DUMP-FAIL per artifact, the #78 120 s budget + stderr capture on both); the DUMP-RETRY=/DUMP-FAIL lines gain the artifact relFile; the hook returns `{ ok, files, error }` (files = the landed paths, md first; error = `<md|json>: <detail>` per failed artifact, joined by \" | \"); the no-overwrite stamp stays MD-base-driven (both artifacts share the stamped base); the dispatch call site reads only ok/error (unchanged). (2) Offscreen platform: a shown zero-area (e.g. 500x0 childless) top-level window never receives a Paint event, and no Expose event is delivered offscreen at all — paint-based triggers need a non-zero area; QWindow has no exposed signal bound in PySide6. (3) An explicit resize() on a top-level widget with a layout PERMANENTLY disables auto-resize to the layout sizeHint (top-level auto-resize tracks sizeHint only until an explicit resize) — children then get clipped; probed: window stayed 500x30 instead of following a 30px label in a previously auto-sized window. (4) First build of a widget with a custom font/stylesheet pays the process-wide first font/stylesheet cost (18.6 ms cold vs 1.1 ms warm offscreen) — pre-payable by building one throwaway widget at startup.\n21: \n\n(End of file - total 21 lines)\n</content>\n(42% used, 134K left)"

  [tool output] "<path>C:\\Users\\Wasiejen\\Repos\\opencode-agent\\projects\\Free-Snap-Tap\\repo\\repo_gotchas.md</path>\n<type>file</type>\n<content>\n1: # repo_gotchas.md — gotchas part of `repo_overview.md` (split 2026-09-11)\n2: \n3: Sections moved verbatim from the former root file; the overview (`repo_overview.md`, this folder) is now a\n4: thin index pointing at the parts.\n5: \n6: ## Gotchas\n7: - `Config_Manager.load_config` opens `self._file_name` directly — point it at a\n8:   `tmp_path` fixture or monkeypatch `_open_config_file`.\n9: - `presort_lines` receives the **cleaned** lines (no spaces after commas inside\n10:   key groups) — feed cleaned-form strings in tests.\n11: - `Output_Manager.execute_key_event` is `async` and calls `asyncio.sleep` +\n12:   pynput — mock both when testing.\n13: - `CONSTANTS` is a real module-level class used as a global config holder; tests\n14:   that mutate it should restore it.\n15: - The repo's `FSTconfig_test.txt` is the maintainer's live config —\n16:   `tests/test_config_parse.py::test_real_config_parses` uses it as a regression\n17:   guard.\n18: - PySide6 `destroyed` gotcha: a handler connected to `obj.destroyed` ALSO fires\n19:   when OTHER objects are destroyed, and the signal argument is an untrusted\n20:   placeholder (a bare QWidget) — capture the object in the closure and probe\n21:   liveness (any C++ method call raises RuntimeError once the C++ side is\n22:   deleted).\n23: - pytest-qt quirks: `QTest.mouseMove(widget, pos)` takes a LOCAL position\n24:   (global = widget.pos() + pos); double-click is `qtbot.mouseDClick` (NOT\n25:   `mouseDoubleClick`); `QSystemTrayIcon` is a QObject, not a QWidget →\n26:   `qtbot.addWidget` rejects it.\n27: - Offscreen destruction ordering: `destroyed` fires while the dying widget's item\n28:   is still in its parent layout — never assert layout-count-based visibility\n29:   right after deletion; use the dict (synchronous source of truth).\n30: - NEVER call a widget's `contextMenuEvent` in tests (its `exec_` blocks the\n31:   loop); trigger the `QAction`s of `widget.context_menu` via `.trigger()`\n32:   instead.\n33: - Edit tool `replaceAll` semantics (probe-verified 2026-09-10, P05): SINGLE\n34:   PASS over the ORIGINAL string — the replaced output is never re-scanned\n35:   (rename `kb_env`→`kb_env_ns` does NOT cascade into `kb_env_ns_ns…`). It\n36:   still matches SUBSTRINGS inside longer tokens (`kb_envx`→`kb_env_nsx`),\n37:   and if the TARGET name already exists in the file, its source-name prefix\n38:   is corrupted in the same pass (`kb_env_ns`→`kb_env_ns_ns`) — that is the\n39:   only case needing the two-stage token dance or a scripted whole-file\n40:   substitution; otherwise a direct rename is safe in one edit (verify with a\n41:   grep afterward).\n42: - Host-dependent capability checks (built-in modules, runtime flags, bundled\n43:   binaries) must probe the REAL host (P06): inside the plugin/process or with\n44:   the bundled runtime — never a same-named system CLI (the system-bun vs.\n45:   opencode.exe-bun false confidence, #37).\n\n(End of file - total 45 lines)\n</content>\n(49% used, 118K left)"

A build spanning several\n4:   layers/files is a LIST of ordered tasks, never one big spec. Each task\n5:   must be completable and verifiable in a comfortable fraction of the\n6:   worker's context.\n7: - **Never mandate broad research in a spec.** If a shape is already\n8:   demonstrated in the repo (maintainer prototype, existing code, a\n9:   committed reference file), the spec says \"build on that shape\" — never\n10:   \"verify the SDK from the npm type defs\". A verification step is either\n11:   small + bounded (one named file, one question) or a separate task.\n12:   Extensive re-research of settled facts is what burned the previous\n13:   worker run (5–6 context cycles, zero work done).\n14: - **The spec is a contract, not an essay:** exact scope, exact DoD\n15:   (measurable end states — probe count, grep-clean, byte-exact format),\n16:   and an explicit **DO-NOT-touch** list (including the maintainer's live\n17:    files and anything under `maintainer/`). Pin the WHAT and the\n18:   end state; the HOW is the worker's call inside the DoD.\n19: - **Spec size is context.** Keep specs short (usually < 100 lines).\n20:   Every spec line competes with the work for the worker's window.\n21: - **Order + green state:** tasks are ordered so each one leaves the repo\n22:   green; each spec is written against the just-verified state (fresh\n23:   line numbers / reference sets / baselines) and is COMMITTED BEFORE the\n24:   launch — a spec is never launched uncommitted.\n25: - **Spec wave (approved 2026-09-24, maintainer ruling — recommended method):**\n26:   when context allows, pre-write a QUEUE of committed specs as MULTIPLE files\n27:   (e.g. `handover/specs/` + a README naming each spec + the queue order)\n28:   instead of one spec at a time. Each spec still follows every rule above\n29:   (committed before launch, written against the just-verified state); the\n30:   launching planner (fresh or post-compaction) launches from the committed\n31:   files. Rationale: generating many specs at once is cheaper than re-deriving\n32:   them later — adapting a committed spec costs less context than re-deriving\n33:   it for the next or a compacted planner. Use it after a design session that\n34:   settles a multi-item build.\n35: - **Claims in a spec are the planner's verified facts** (measured at spec\n36:   time), not assignments for the worker to re-derive.\n37: - **Flag behaviour-pinning tests explicitly:** the spec's verified-facts\n38:   section must name existing tests that pin the OBSERVABLE behaviour the\n39:   change alters, and say that repurposing them is in-scope (worker-40\n40:   friction, 2026-09-30: a \"zero test grep hits\" fact missed a test pinning\n41:   the old rebind-repeat behaviour — the worker had to infer the scope).\n42: - **Full repo-relative paths for file references:** name proposal / spec /\n43:   knowledge files by their FULL repo-relative path (`proposals/\n44:   approved/…`, not `proposals/approved/…`) — a bare name costs the worker a\n45:   find/glob call (2026-09-26/27 frictions).\n46: - **Probe line refs point at the CODE block, re-verified at authoring:**\n47:   when the spec cites a probe case by line range, point at the case's code\n48:   block (never the header annotation) and re-verify the range + the\n49:   baseline counts against the probe's header tally at spec time (28-line-\n50:   short ranges + a stale baseline count cost worker calls, 2026-09-26/27).\n51: - **Live-acceptance specs open with a cheap build canary:** before the\n52:   acceptance battery, trigger one known current-behavior line and confirm\n53:   it appears in intercept.log — the live process can LAG HEAD after a mid-\n54:   incident restart (the 2026-09-26_14-26 case burned ~12 attempts); the\n55:   planner re-verifies the live process state after ANY mid-incident\n56:   restart, not just at session start.\n57: - **No \"no new TODO entry expected\" DoD assumptions:** state the expectation,\n58:   never promise it — a fix may legitimately surface a doc/code discrepancy\n59:   that gets filed (plan25 friction, 2026-09-27).\n60: - **Commit hash in the DoD (codified 2026-09-22 after two worker stumbles):**\n61:   a worker's commit can NEVER contain its own hash — the hash exists only\n62:   AFTER the commit, and the TODO/handover files ride in that same commit.\n63:   A DoD that needs the hash says: status → `LANDED` (hash recorded in the\n64:   planner's follow-up bookkeeping commit); the planner records the hash in\n65:   its own bookkeeping commit (the #80 precedent: 1fd3726 recorded 4098253).\n66:   Never write \"LANDED (commit hash)\" as a same-commit requirement — it is\n67:   unsatisfiable by construction.\n68: - **One-line shape notes for nested fields:** when the spec names a data\n69:   shape, state the nesting explicitly (e.g. `info.tokens: {input, output,\n70:   cache.read}` — the tokens live INSIDE entry.info, not at the entry top\n71:   level) — prevents double-drafted wrong-shape fixtures (knowledge_inbox\n72:   2026-09-27_00-15 lesson).\n73: - **Checkpoint commits (approved 2026-09-25 — the one-commit loosening, his\n74:   #2026-09-24_22-47):** the worker commits ONE green checkpoint per verified\n75:   unit (code only — the commit routine's append-discrepancies rule still\n76:   applies to each), so an interrupted session loses at most one unit;\n77:   `TODO.md` + the handover file ride the FINAL commit (carrying the code\n78:   commits' hashes, never their own). Spec DoDs state \"checkpoint commits per\n79:   verified unit; TODO + handover in the final commit\" — never \"ONE commit\"\n80:   for the whole task. AGENTS.md §Commit-routine step 1 carries the same rule\n81:   (maintainer paste pending — this bullet + the spec DoD line govern until\n82:   it lands).\n\n(End of file - total 82 lines)\n</content>\n(53% used, 110K left)"

A DB write therefore changes the next\n17: prompt with zero restart, zero invalidation, zero host cooperation.\n18: \n19: ## The two levers (research doc §5)\n20: \n21: 1. **tail** (preferred, non-destructive): rewrite the compaction part's\n22:    `tail_start_id` — the host does exactly this itself on compaction\n23:    (compaction.ts:461-466). One JSON field on one part row trims or extends\n24:    the retained tail precisely (`result.slice(tailIndex, compactionIndex)`,\n25:    message-v2.ts:567-571). Reversible (point it back at the old id). Only\n26:    usable when a compaction marker exists.\n27: 2. **turns** (destructive): delete complete finished old turns (user row +\n28:    its assistant rows; parts cascade via FK, `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON`)\n29:    strictly BEFORE the current last user message and strictly OUTSIDE the\n30:    compaction-marker quartet (compaction user row + compaction part +\n31:    summary row + `tail_start_id` target). The only lever for sessions with\n32:    NO marker.\n33: \n34: ## Proposed surface\n35: \n36: - `report <session> <target>` — DRY RUN (default mode): what a trim to the\n37:   target would remove (message count + token mass via the gauge's S metric\n38:   or bytes/4 fallback) and what would remain; no write.\n39: - `tail <session> <messageID>` — apply the `tail_start_id` rewrite after\n40:   validating the id exists, sits before the compaction user row, and keeps\n41:   the window guard (message-v2.ts:568) satisfiable; single transaction.\n42: - `turns <session> <target|count>` — apply the old-turn deletions after\n43:   validating every row against the safe-zone rules (no last user message,\n44:   no quartet, no in-flight rows); single transaction.\n45: \n46: Validation mirrors the window guard exactly; fail-closed on any doubt.\n47: WAL coordination: a brief write lock; the host reads on its own connection\n48: (SQLite WAL = concurrent read, single writer).\n49: \n50: ## Hazards (research doc §4-5)\n51: \n52: - Deleting the last user message makes the next prompt re-run the OLDEST\n53:   surviving user turn (MessageV2.latest, message-v2.ts:586-602).\n54: - Deleting ANY marker-quartet member degenerates the window to the FULL\n55:   history → overflow, and this host has NO auto-compact backstop\n56:   (`compaction.auto = false`).\n57: - Row surgery near the session tail or the marker is structurally load-bearing.\n58: - `turns` is irreversible (the pre-compaction dumps only cover compacted\n59:   sessions).\n60: \n61: ## Effort\n62: \n63: - Tool ~150-250 lines + DB write coordination; smokes against a FIXTURE DB\n64:   (never the live one); probe pins per the established pattern; registration\n65:   in `opencode.jsonc` (you); live acceptance on a dedicated throwaway session\n66:   (report first, never trim a working session blind).\n67: - ~0.5-1 day worker time (one unit) + one post-restart live-acceptance cycle.\n68: \n69: ## Approval needed\n70: \n71: Live-DB writes + a new tool surface = observable behavior change → your\n72: call. No sugarcoating:\n232: \n233: ### 1. \"no auto-compact backstop\" — your correction is right; my claim was overstated\n234: I was talking about the host-native setting (`compaction.auto = false`). You're right\n235: that context_recovery is the live backstop (live-verified in #93: overflow → recovery\n236: fires → compacts → session continues). So a wrongly-set `tail_start_id` that retains\n237: TOO MUCH → overflow → a second compaction fires. Cost: the first summary's work is\n238: wasted + one re-summarization cycle (~3-4 min) + a fresh marker. Self-healing, not\n239: catastrophic.\n240: One honest caveat: the backstop covers only the overflow direction. So the fail-closed validation (id exists, strictly before the\n243: compaction user row, summary after it, window guard satisfiable) stays the PRIMARY\n244: safety; the backstop is the net for what validation misses in the dangerous direction.\n245: The proposal record gets amended accordingly.\n246: \n247: ### 2. keepMessages floor — agreed\n248: Min 6, stated in the tool description. It's a code constant + one description line.\n249: \n250: ### 3. TUI cost (a) — understood; nothing to build here\n251: The TUI already renders the full DB history (your \"clear log of earlier messages\"\n252: already exists). The only gap is that the TUI shows more than the model sees — you\n253: rate that acceptable / a small feature, not a detriment. I agree there's nothing to\n254: build on this track; a visual \"effective window\" indicator would be a separate small\n255: feature if you ever want it.\n256: \n257: ### 4. Self-prune as a deliberate tool — agreed, and it argues for item 3 EARLIER than I gated it\n258: Your use-case sketch (drop big tool outputs — webfetch findings — while preserving\n259: loaded repo/readme state) is exactly what per-message toggling solves. Candid note:\n260: the approved scope (1+2) does NOT cover it at fine granularity. `report` gives the\n261: informed picker (which messages are big, which files/lines); `tail` gives only the\n262: COARSE boundary (everything before it is replaced by the summary). \"Drop just this\n263: one webfetch output, keep the rest\" needs the state-list fork (item 3). So your\n264: self-prune idea is the strongest argument to promote item 3 from \"only if proven\n265: needed\" to \"planned follow-up\". With your own-fork learning motive on top, my order\n266: is now: 1+2 now (approved), 3 next as the learning-fork unit.\n267: \n268: ### The \"28 thousand forks\" pushback — you're right, and the npm point was over-flagged\n269: Fork maintenance is normal practice; your workflow (fetch origin → merge → move\n270: keep-out changes → rebuild) is what those 28k forks do — nothing new or hard in it.\n271: The npm point, precisely: the installed host is a global npm install\n272: (`AppData\\Roaming\\npm\\node_modules\\opencode-ai\\bin\\opencode.exe`) and the fork exe is\n273: SWAPPED into that path. The only hazard: a later `npm update opencode-ai` (or\n274: reinstall) silently overwrites the swapped fork with upstream — the fork dies\n275: unnoticed. npm install itself is NOT the problem; npm UPDATE clobbers the swap. And\n276: under the workflow you describe (updates go fork-side: fetch → merge → rebuild →\n277: swap), the concern evaporates entirely. One standing rule: never `npm update` that\n278: package while the fork is in place. So: a non-issue in your workflow, as you say.\n279: \n280: ### 5. Model toggling — an idea, not buildable now; feasibility hinges on one research question\n281: Candidly, it has three parts: (a) per-session model choice — possible TODAY (sessions\n282: spawn with a chosen agent+model; auto_resume already resolves the identity to\n283: inject); (b) mid-session per-step switching — plausibly feasible: #80's live finding\n284: (2026-09-22) shows the host runs a turn as the agent+model carried in that message's\n285: `info` (the injected turns ran as \"build\" until the info was fixed) — so a plugin\n286: rewriting the next user message's model before the prefill (the same DB-write\n287: mechanism as the tail lever) would switch the turn's model; the open question is the\n288: exact read point + a clean write moment; (c) the trigger policy (\"when the session\n289: slows\") — a heuristic over ctx% + measured t/s; there is no clean \"slowing\" signal\n290: today. All the info I need is now in place. Unit 2 design (from the proposal, approved with "go :-)", plan1's VERDICT note):

**Unit 2 — Post-compaction tail setting (plugin side, no fork needed):**
- Trigger: existing verified channel — the `COMPACT` line in ctx.log (= host-side compaction landing).
- Read the DB and locate the new compaction part + its `tail_start_id`, compute the keepMessages boundary (the tool's existing count metric — i.e. the keepMessages value from the compact_memory dispatch), and rewrite the id.
- Same validation as Unit 1's `tail` (the id must exist, strictly before the compaction user row, summary after it, window guard satisfiable, floor 6).
- Log `tail-set= old→new keep=N`.
- "The integration point (polling vs inline-after-dispatch) will be pinned in the task spec."

So the spec has to pin the integration point. Where does the plugin detect the COMPACT line? auto_resume.ts's tick already tail-reads `.opencode/temp/ctx.log` for new `COMPACT <sid>` lines (that's #98 Part B — `tailCompactRearm`: for WATCHED sids: idlePending=true + recoveryCount=0). So the natural integration point is: extend auto_resume's tick (or the compact_memory dispatch path?).

Two candidate integration points:
1. **auto_resume.ts tick** (already reads ctx.log COMPACT lines — the `tailCompactRearm` leg): on a new COMPACT line for the sid, after re-arming, read the DB (that session's latest compaction part + tail_start_id), compute the keepMessages boundary, and rewrite. This is the "polling" approach — fires on the next 5-second tick after compaction lands.
2. **compact_memory.ts dispatch path** (inline after dispatch): after a successful SELF/CROSS dispatch (COMPACT line lands), do the DB read + rewrite inline. But wait — the COMPACT line is written by… let me think. Who writes the COMPACT line in ctx.log? The ctx_gauge plugin (gauge's write on compaction event?). Hmm. Actually the COMPACT line in ctx.log: per the compact_memory/ctx system, ctx.log records `COMPACT <sid>` lines (the auto_resume #98 Part B tick tail-reads ctx.log for "new COMPACT <sid> lines"). Where is the COMPACT line written? Probably the gauge plugin (ctx_watchdog) on a compaction event, or the compact_memory plugin on successful dispatch. The proposal says "the existing verified channel (ctx.log COMPACT line = host-side compaction landing)".

Also key: keepMessages boundary — "compute the keepMessages boundary (the tool's existing count metric)". The compact_memory dispatch already computes the keepTokens (S-diff metric) from the last keepMessages messages. The boundary = the ID of the first message in the keepMessages window. So the plugin needs: sid, keepMessages count (from where? the dispatch call — the tool arg; or the default from the budget file keepMessages: 18? the budget file has keepMessages + keepTokens). For SELF compactions triggered by the compact_memory tool, keepMessages is a dispatch arg. For auto-compactions… the auto-compact backstop (context_recovery) also compacts. The COMPACT line is a generic host-side compaction landing channel.

Hmm — for the tail-set to work generically, keepMessages has to be resolved: the dispatch arg (when known), else the budget file's `keepMessages` (fallback), else the host default? Design decision to pin in the spec.

Also: timing — the tail rewrite must happen "before the next step's prefill" (the host re-reads the DB fresh at the top of every loop step). A 5-second tick after the COMPACT line: compaction lands → session continues (post-compaction first message) → the next prefill happens immediately. Race: does the tick fire before or after the next prefill? The COMPACT line is written when the compaction part is committed (host-side). After compaction, the host's next loop step rebuilds the context — probably within seconds. The 5-second tick could miss the first post-compaction prefill. Hmm — that's why the proposal says "before the next step's prefill" (Q2 discussion) — and says "no race, no restart: the host re-reads the DB fresh at the top of every loop step — the next prefill uses the rewritten tail." If the rewrite lands 5-10 seconds late, the first post-compaction prefill uses the host's default tail (40k token budget), and subsequent prefills use the rewritten tail. Slightly suboptimal but functionally fine? Or, inline-after-dispatch (on the SELF compact_memory path) happens before the host continues… actually, wait — how does the SELF compact_memory dispatch work? The plugin calls the SDK's summarize (client.session.summarize?) — that's what triggers the host compaction. The dispatch is fire-and-forget… the host's summarize request resolves, and only then is the compaction part + summary + tail_start_id written. The plugin's dispatch returns before the host has written (async). So inline-after-dispatch would need to wait for the compaction part to appear in the DB (polling the DB) — possible: poll the DB until the new compaction part appears (bounded, e.g. 30s), then rewrite the tail.

Hmm, this is a design decision. The proposal says "the integration point (polling vs inline-after-dispatch) will be pinned in the task spec." I have to pin it. Let me think about which is best:

Option A (auto_resume tick, poll ctx.log COMPACT line):
+ Reuses the existing verified channel + tick infra (the ctx.log tail read, the ctxLogOffset cursor already exist — #98 Part B).
+ Covers all compactions (self via compact_memory, the context_recovery backstop, host auto if it ever happens) — not just our dispatch.
+ Simple: in the tick, for a new COMPACT line (any sid, or only watched? — all sids? the tail-set is per session; should apply to any session that just compacted — including workers), read the DB for that sid's latest compaction part, compute the boundary, rewrite.
− 5-second tick latency: the first post-compaction prefill may use the host tail; the rewrite applies from the second prefill. Is that a problem? The whole point is exact keepMessages retention. Missing the first prefill = one step at the host's 40k-budget tail (maybe larger than keepMessages' token size). Functionally OK (a transient), and the #98 re-arm already happens on the same tick — the recovery/continue flow sends a message after… hmm, actually the tick order: tailCompactRearm sets idlePending=true before the routing loop. Then recovery-continue fires (promptAsync) → that's a new step → prefill! So the tick's tail-set has to run before the recovery-continue send within the same tick. Order: tailCompactRearm (re-arm) → limitStopCheck → routing loop (which may send recovery-continue). If I add the tail-set inside the tailCompactRearm leg (synchronous DB read + rewrite before idlePending processing), it completes before the routing loop sends the continue.

Option B (inline after dispatch in compact_memory.ts):
+ Zero latency for SELF dispatches (poll the DB for the compaction part, rewrite immediately — before the host's next prefill).
− Only covers our dispatch (SELF/CROSS compact_memory calls), not the context_recovery backstop or host auto-compaction.
− Need to add DB polling to compact_memory.ts (new dependency; the tool currently doesn't read the DB… actually it does — computeKeepTokens reads the DB! The S-diff metric reads the session's messages from the DB. So compact_memory.ts already has the DB read infra).
− Race: the dispatch is fire-and-forget; the tool's execute returns right after the dispatch (per the design — "a CROSS dispatch is fire-and-forget: return immediately"). For SELF: the session ends after compaction (the SELF path's compacted session ends after compaction; a message may be queued). The tool returns before the host writes the compaction part. Inline-after-dispatch would need async waiting (a background poll after the tool returns) — feasible (a setImmediate loop in the plugin process), but adds complexity + lifecycle questions (what if the host restarts? the compaction never lands?).

Hybrid: do it in the auto_resume tick (Option A) — covers all paths, reuses the verified channel, and the tick is already the coordination point (re-arm on COMPACT line). The 5-second latency concern is resolved by running the tail-set inside the tailCompactRearm leg before the routing loop's send. This is my pin.

Wait — one more consideration: which keepMessages value? For a SELF compact_memory dispatch, the dispatch arg keepMessages (the tool arg) is the agent's intent. Is that recorded anywhere persistent? The COMPACT line in ctx.log — what does it carry? Per the #99 close note: "COMPACT line (keep=12m tok=29498 computed)" — the COMPACT line carries keep=Nm (keepMessages count) + tok= (computed tokens)! So the COMPACT line itself carries the keepMessages count (from the dispatch). For host auto-compactions or context_recovery, the line may carry the default. So: parse the keep= field from the COMPACT line; fallback = the budget file's keepMessages; else omit the tail-set (no rewrite). Let me verify the COMPACT line format — I should check what writes the COMPACT line. The gauge plugin (ctx_watchdog / gauge.mjs)? Let me grep for the ctx.log COMPACT writer. Also, the keepTokens computation reads the DB — in compact_memory.ts or compaction_core.ts (shared after #105 compaction-unification: "shared compaction_core.ts + thin wrappers").

I need a few verified facts for the spec:
1. Who writes the `COMPACT <sid>` line in ctx.log + its exact format (does it carry keep= / tok=?).
2. Where the S-diff keepTokens computation lives (compaction_core.ts) + how the keepMessages window boundary (first message id) is derived — can the tail-set reuse it? The computation returns the token mass, but the tail-set needs the first message ID of the last N messages. The computeKeepTokens logic probably enumerates the last N messages — it has their ids.
3. Where the auto_resume tick's tailCompactRearm leg is (line number) + how ctxLogOffset works.
4. The context_trim tool's tail validation code (to reuse/mirror: the fail-closed validation function — can the plugin import it? The tool is .opencode/tools/context_trim.ts — a standalone tool. The plugin is .opencode/plugin/. Sharing code between tool and plugin — probably no import path; the plugin will mirror the validation (same as #104's S31 equivalence pattern). Next, the Qt/PySide knowledge curation → repo_gotchas.md (FST). Append 4 facts (condensed):
- Qt/PySide6 offscreen + first-show facts (2026-10-01, worker-4 #130, measured offscreen): (1) `QWidget.windowHandle()` is None until first `show()` (it doesn't create the window); a `show()` before the event loop creates the native handle synchronously, and the handle persists after `hide()` — re-showing a hidden top-level is cheap. (2) Offscreen: a zero-area (e.g., 500x0 childless) top-level window never receives Paint, and offscreen there are no Expose events at all — paint-based triggers need non-zero area; PySide6 has no exposed QWindow signal. (3) An explicit `resize()` on a top-level with a layout permanently disables auto-resize to layout sizeHint (top-level auto-resize tracks sizeHint only until an explicit resize) — children get clipped. (4) The first build of a widget with a custom font/stylesheet pays the process-wide first font/stylesheet cost (measured 18.6ms cold vs 1.1ms warm offscreen) — pre-payable by building one throwaway widget at startup.

And the curation-log line in knowledge_inbox.md:
- 2026-10-01 (plan5 maintenance pass): "PySide6/Qt offscreen + first-show facts (2026-10-01_05-58, worker-4 #130)" → `projects/Free-Snap-Tap/repo/repo_gotchas.md` (4 bullet facts).

```
# orientation.md — Free-Snap-Tap: where the project is going

Planner-authored distillation (2026-10-01, plan5 maintenance pass) of the
maintainer's goal sketch (ideas.md 2026-09-30 "goal overview for each Repo") +
the settled FST facts. The NAP owns the CURRENT state; this file owns the
DIRECTION. Explicit maintainer instructions override both.

## What FST is
The snap-tap / rebind / macro tool — the product code (stable; gates green).
Bookkeeping + repo docs: this folder (`TODO.md`, `todo_records.md`, `repo/`).

## Direction (maintainer goal sketch, ideas.md 2026-09-30)
- **Updates always on `opencode_test`** — always checked out, directly
  testable by the maintainer.
- **Input lag is the standing goal:** always find and resolve cases that
  would lead to input lag — a failing part must NEVER impede general
  responsiveness (no blocking, no lag on failure). The first start of any
  repeat may be a bit laggy (acceptable, pre-warm candidates — see #130).
- **Keep the wiki + readme current** so new versions can be published —
  on a successful rework, a new version (e.g. v1.3.0) is published on GitHub
  as a new release; the issue-tracker access may be granted to find/fix
  problems directly.
- **Config support direction** (ideas.md 2026-09-30 "FST config file
  check"): the near-term step = a config validation function set (syntactic
  + semantic checks, pipeable independent of the full program — watch the
  logic-doubling cost) and/or a small GUI (tabs per focus group, config-
  file picker + live check, display pointing at the wrong parts, the
  currently-pressed key + vk codes from `vk_codes.py`); the grand goal = a
  drag-and-drop config designer (likely impractical — the maintainer rates
  better error diagnostics + hidden-interconnection display as the real win).
  NOT yet a task — a future FST feature (maintainer call).
- **Wacom Touch Driver** (separate repo, no agent access yet): add Pen
  support, improve responsiveness + GUI configurability (rotation of input
  options, multiple connected touch pads), the opentabletdrive repo as
  inspiration source, possibly a vision module to see the GUIs.

## Working conventions (settled facts)
- FST gate = pytest + ruff (see `repo/repo_commands.md`); venv 3.12.9.
- Work branches: `fst_work*` (current `fst_work3`, off `opencode_test`);
  `fst_work` is the parked rebuild branch (historical).
- Live testing / live display = maintainer domain; agents verify via the
  test gate + offscreen probes.
- FST TODO entries live in this folder's `TODO.md` (IDs continue the global
  sequence — the counter in the root `TODO.md`).
```

```
# Task spec — TODO #120 Unit 2: the plugin-side post-compaction tail-set (zero fork)

## Goal
After any host compaction lands, the auto_resume plugin rewrites the fresh
compaction part's `tail_start_id` to the EXACT keepMessages boundary — exact
count-based retention (the last keepMessages pre-compaction messages),
replacing the host's token-budget tail semantics. Pre-approved class
(agent-usage; the approved proposal `proposals/implemented/
2026-09-28_context-trim-tool.md` Unit 2 + his "1. + 2. are approved" / "go :-)").

## Verified facts (measured 2026-10-01, plan5)
- The ctx.log COMPACT line format (compaction_core.ts L328-346, L356+):
  `YYYY-MM-DD_HH-MM [model] COMPACT <sid> keep=<m>m tok=<t> <source>
  [ emergency] [ ovr] ...` — `keep=<m>m` is ALWAYS present (the dispatch
  keep arg WHEN GIVEN, else the config keepMessages, default 12).
- The auto_resume tick order (auto_resume.ts L1631-1636): `tailCompactRearm()`
  → `limitStopCheck()` → the routing loop. `tailCompactRearm()` (L1481-1523)
  tail-reads ctx.log from the `ctxLogOffset` cursor (L319); per NEW
  `COMPACT <sid>` line with a WATCHED sid it sets `lastCompactAt` /
  `idlePending` / `recoveryCount=0` + the `rearm= compact sid=` log;
  `COMPACT_SID_RE` (L1465) captures only the sid.
- The keepMessages window at dispatch (compaction_core.ts `computeKeepTokens`
  L141-154): `msgs.slice(max(0, len - keepMessages))` — the last N messages of
  the PRE-compaction list. At tick time the post-compaction raw list ends at
  the summary row — the boundary MUST be computed over the messages strictly
  BEFORE the compaction user row (take the last N of that prefix; boundary =
  the first of them's id).
- The landed `tail` lever (context_trim.ts): FLOOR = 6 (L102); the fail-closed
  validation verdicts (`ok` / `rejected: ...` incl. `rejected:
  retained-tail-below-floor N`); the write (L632): `UPDATE part SET data =
  json_set(data, '$.tail_start_id', ?), time_updated = ? WHERE id = ?` —
  single transaction; the DB backend chain: in-process sqlite (bun:sqlite →
  node:sqlite) first, fallback the maintainer-placed sqlite3 CLI (async
  execFile, one CLI call per query, the tail transaction = ONE call, the #126
  hardening: \x01 separator, 16 MB buffer). The live host process has NO
  working in-process sqlite (#37/#126) → the live path is the CLI fallback.
- Baseline (plan4, 2026-10-01): probe 352/352 (341 PASS + the 11 #113
  env-fails 136-146); auto_resume smoke 152/152; compact_memory 89/89;
  context_trim 20/20; all 11 smokes green.

## Design (pinned per the approved proposal)
- Integration point: the auto_resume 5s tick — a NEW tail-set leg running
  AFTER `tailCompactRearm()`, BEFORE `limitStopCheck()`. `tailCompactRearm`
  is refactored to ALSO collect the NEW COMPACT lines (ALL sids, not only
  watched — worker sessions benefit too): sid + the parsed `keep=` value.
  Its existing behavior + the `rearm=` log lines stay byte-identical (the
  152/152 pins must not change).
- Per NEW COMPACT line (fail-open, never throws; own try/catch):
  1. keep = the line's `keep=<n>` value; fallback = the budget file's
     `keepMessages` (the config default 12) when the field is absent/
     unparseable; still unavailable → skip + log.
  2. DB read (the backend chain above): the session's messages + the last
     COMPLETED compaction (a user-role message with a `compaction` part
     carrying `tail_start_id !== undefined`) + its summary child.
  3. Boundary: over the messages strictly BEFORE the compaction user row
     ((time_created, id) order), the last `keep` → boundary id = the first
     of them.
  4. Validate (MIRROR the context_trim.ts tail validation, fail-closed):
     session exists; last completed compaction exists (marker + summary
     child); boundary id exists in the session; boundary strictly BEFORE the
     compaction user row; retained-tail count (boundary → compaction row,
     exclusive) >= FLOOR 6. Any doubt → NO write + log the rejection.
  5. Write: the single-transaction `json_set` UPDATE (same form as
     context_trim.ts L632) — the host re-derives the context from the DB at
     the top of EVERY loop step (no restart; the first post-compaction
     prefill may still use the host's own tail — the rewrite applies from
     the next step; a too-large retention self-heals via the
     context_recovery backstop, the proposal feedback §1).
  6. Log (auto_resume.log, ONE line per processed COMPACT line):
     `tail-set= <old> -> <new> keep=<N> sid=<sid>` (rejection form:
     `tail-set= rejected: <reason> sid=<sid>`).
- Code placement: a NEW module file in `.opencode/plugin/` (e.g.
  `context_tail.ts`) called from the tick — or inline in auto_resume.ts;
  the worker's call inside the DoD. If the validation/backend is EXTRACTED
  to a shared module imported by context_trim.ts too: the tool's observable
  behavior + ALL its pins stay unchanged (probe S33 + the 20/20 smoke green);
  mirroring inline (no refactor) is the lower-risk default.
- DO-NOT-touch: the restart/spawn path, the nudge ladder, `limitStopCheck`,
  compact_memory.ts dispatch, context_trim.ts's observable behavior,
  `maintainer/**`, the live opencode.db (smokes = FIXTURE DB only),
  opencode.jsonc (no registration change — auto_resume is a live plugin).

## Definition of done
- New `auto_resume.smoke.mjs` section (fixture DB — the in-process sqlite
  path works in the smoke process):
  (a) NEW COMPACT line keep=N + a fixture state (compaction part + summary +
  N+3 pre-compaction messages) → the part's tail_start_id rewritten to the
  boundary id + the `tail-set= old -> new keep=N sid=` line;
  (b) a boundary whose retained tail < 6 → NO write + the rejection line;
  (c) compaction part WITHOUT a summary child → skip + line, NO write;
  (d) a COMPACT line with no `keep=` field → the budget-file keepMessages
  fallback;
  (e) no NEW COMPACT line → ZERO DB access (a cursor/call-counter pin) and
  the existing 152/152 pins stay green (the `rearm=` lines unchanged).
- New probe section (S34) mirroring the key smoke pins; the probe total
  352 → 352+N (the header self-annotated; the 11 #113 env-fails unchanged).
- Standard gate green: the probe + all 11 smokes (auto_resume > 152;
  compact_memory 89/89; context_trim 20/20; the rest at baseline).
- Checkpoint commits per verified unit; `TODO.md` (#120 status: Unit 2
  LANDED — the hash rides the planner's follow-up bookkeeping commit) +
  this handover in the FINAL commit.
- No new registration (no opencode.jsonc change). Live acceptance = the next
  real compaction shows the `tail-set=` line in auto_resume.log (natural
  occurrence) — noted in the TODO status, not a DoD item.
```

  [tool input] {"filePath":"C:\\Users\\Wasiejen\\Repos\\opencode-agent\\projects\\Free-Snap-Tap\\repo\\repo_gotchas.md","oldString":"- Host-dependent capability checks (built-in modules, runtime flags, bundled\n  binaries) must probe the REAL host (P06): inside the plugin/process or with\n  the bundled runtime — never a same-named system CLI (the system-bun vs.\n  opencode.exe-bun false confidence, #37).","newString":"- Host-dependent capability checks (built-in modules, runtime flags, bundled\n  binaries) must probe the REAL host (P06): inside the plugin/process or with\n  the bundled runtime — never a same-named system CLI (the system-bun vs.\n  opencode.exe-bun false confidence, #37).\n- Qt/PySide6 offscreen + first-show facts (verified 2026-10-01, worker-4\n  #130, measured offscreen): (1) `QWidget.windowHandle()` is None until the\n  widget has been shown at least once (it does NOT create the window); a\n  `show()` BEFORE the event loop creates the native handle synchronously and\n  the handle persists after `hide()` — re-showing a hidden top-level window is\n  cheap (the #130 pre-warm relies on this). (2) Offscreen: a shown zero-area\n  (e.g. 500x0 childless) top-level window never receives a Paint event, and\n  no Expose event is delivered offscreen at all — paint-based triggers need a\n  non-zero area; QWindow has no exposed signal bound in PySide6. (3) An\n  explicit `resize()` on a top-level widget WITH a layout PERMANENTLY disables\n  auto-resize to the layout sizeHint (top-level auto-resize tracks sizeHint\n  only until the first explicit resize) — children then get clipped. (4) The\n  first build of a widget with a custom font/stylesheet pays the\n  process-wide first font/stylesheet cost (18.6 ms cold vs 1.1 ms warm\n  offscreen) — pre-payable by building one throwaway widget at startup."}

Explicit maintainer instructions override both.\n\n## What FST is\nThe snap-tap / rebind / macro tool — the product code (stable, gates green).\nPer-project bookkeeping + repo docs live in THIS folder (`TODO.md`,\n`todo_records.md`, `repo/`).\n\n## Direction (maintainer goal sketch, ideas.md 2026-09-30)\n- **Updates always on `opencode_test`** — always checked out, directly\n  testable by the maintainer.\n- **Input lag is the standing goal:** always find and resolve cases that\n  would lead to input lag — a failing part must NEVER impede general\n  responsiveness (no blocking, no lag when it fails). The first start of any\n  repeat may be a bit laggy (acceptable; first-call pre-warm candidates —\n  see #130).\n- **Keep the wiki + readme current** so new versions can be published —\n  once a successful rework is tested, a new version (e.g. v1.3.0) is\n  published on GitHub as a new release; the issue-tracker access may be\n  granted to find problems and fix them directly.\n- **Config support direction** (his ideas.md 2026-09-30 \"FST config file\n  check\"): near-term = a config validation function set (syntactic +\n  semantic checks, pipeable independent of the complete program — he\n  flags the logic-doubling cost: it must be maintained alongside each code\n  change) and/or a small GUI (tabs per focus group, a config-file picker +\n  live check without loading, displays pointing at the wrong parts, and the\n  currently-pressed key with its vk code from `vk_codes.py`). NOT a task yet — a future FST feature (maintainer\n  call).\n- **Wacom Touch Driver** (separate repo — NO agent access yet): add Pen\n  support; improve responsiveness + configurability via the GUI (rotation of\n  input options, multiple connected touch pads); the opentabletdrive repo\n  as inspiration source; possibly a vision module to let the agent see the\n  GUIs.\n\n## Working conventions (settled facts)\n- FST gate = pytest + ruff (commands in `repo/repo_commands.md`); the repo\n  venv is 3.12.9 (#113 close).\n- Work branches: `fst_work*` (current `fst_work3`, off `opencode_test`);\n  branch truth is verified with `git branch -v` at task start, never from\n  memory.\n- Live testing / the real display = maintainer domain; agents verify via the\n  test gate + offscreen probes (the Qt offscreen facts are in\n  `repo/repo_gotchas.md`).\n- FST TODO entries live in THIS folder's `TODO.md`; IDs continue the global\n  sequence (the counter in the root `TODO.md` header).\n"}

```js
import { readFileSync, writeFileSync } from "fs";

const root = "C:/Users/Wasiejen/Repos/opencode-agent/";
const stale = ["17","30","33","35","37","38","39","40","49","50","51","52","64","68","69","71","72","73"];

// --- TODO.md: remove stale closed one-liners + add #132 + update counter
const tp = root + "TODO.md";
let lines = readFileSync(tp, "utf8").split("\n");
const out = [];
const removed = [];
for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
  const m = lines[i].match(/^## (?:#?)(\d+)\./);
  if (m && stale.includes(m[1])) {
    removed.push(m[1]);
    if (i + 1 < lines.length && lines[i+1].trim() === "") i++; // swallow the trailing blank
    continue;
  }
  out.push(lines[i]);
}
const miss = stale.filter(id => !removed.includes(id));
if (miss.length) { console.error("MISSING STALE IDS: " + miss.join(",")); process.exit(1); }
if (new Set(removed).size !== stale.length) { console.error("DUPLICATE HEADER"); process.exit(1); }
// counter
const before = out.join("\n").match(/used so far up to #\d+, new\s*\n?entries start at #\d+/);
// do the counter replacement explicitly
let txt = out.join("\n");
const c1 = "used so far up to #131, new\nentries start at #132";
if (!txt.includes(c1)) { console.error("COUNTER NOT FOUND"); process.exit(1); }
txt = txt.replace(c1, "used so far up to #132, new\nentries start at #133");
// append #132 at the end (after the last line)
const entry = [
"",
"## #132. (open, 2026-10-01, plan5 maintenance pass — agent_feedback 2026-09-30_16-57; pre-approved class — agent-usage friction) the silent default-cap-1 budget fallback is invisible: an unlisted model row falls back to cap 1 with no marker",
"- **Problem / evidence:** agent_feedback 2026-09-30_16-57 (planner): after the maintainer's model key swap, the row model was no longer listed in `model_budget` → the silent default-cap-1 fallback (compaction_core.ts ~L205-211 — the file has no \"default\" key) → the injected ctx suffix read \"1 compactions left\" while the self-gauge read 5; ~4 tool calls to diagnose.",
"- **Desired outcome:** the default-cap fallback is visible — a marker whenever the cap resolves to the silent default (not an explicit model_budget entry) on the injected budget suffix (and/or a logged line), and/or a documented `default` key convention in compact_budget.json.",
"- **Acceptance criteria:** a fixture with an unlisted model → the marker appears on the injected suffix / logged line; a compact_memory smoke pin for the marker; standard gate green.",
"- **Suggested scope:** `.opencode/plugin/compaction_core.ts` (the budget read), `.opencode/plugin/tests/compact_memory.smoke.mjs`, the budget-file header comment.",
"- **Status:** open (filed 2026-10-01, plan5 maintenance pass — pending delegation; a small pre-approved build candidate)."
];
txt = txt.replace(/\n*$/, "\n") ; // normalize the tail
txt = txt + entry.join("\n") + "\n";
writeFileSync(tp, txt);
console.log("TODO.md removed: " + removed.join(",") + " (count " + removed.length + ")");

// --- todo_records.md: backfill #64 record
const rp = root + "todo_records.md";
let rec = readFileSync(rp, "utf8");
if (rec.includes("## 64.")) { console.log("todo_records already has ## 64. — skip"); }
else {
  rec = rec.replace(/\n*$/, "\n");
  rec += "## 64. (closed 2026-09-16, plan9 planner-direct; backfilled by the plan5 maintenance pass 2026-10-01 — the record was missing) — stale \"84/84\" probe baseline in `.opencode/plugin/README.md` → replaced by the curate-don't-duplicate pointer to the probe's self-annotated header total (the #58 convention).\n";
  writeFileSync(rp, rec);
  console.log("todo_records #64 backfilled");
}
```
